  [G10.4] Vulnerability has a material impact on the recommendation (overlay)

  Potential Concern — severity High

    Report:   The suitability report identifies two foreseeable harms: (1) investment values may fall if the higher agreed risk level (5 vs questionnaire result of 4) does not align with true risk appetite — mitigated by client confirmation of awareness, acknowledgement that funds may go up and down, no reliance on pension for day-to-day living, and regular reviews; (2) switching costs and critical yield — the new plan including adviser charges is 4.24% more expensive, requiring £4,672 outperformance in year one — mitigated by critical yield analysis and adviser belief that 4.8% p.a. growth to age 75 is achievable. The report also notes Power of Attorney would be prudent to arrange. There is no explicit vulnerability assessment section, no record of vulnerability screening outcome for either client, and no reference to communication adjustments, accessible formats, or specific support needs. The extraction confirms /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome are absent from the document as NotPresentInDocument, and /vulnerability/perClient[]/impactOnRecommendation[] is also absent.

    File:     The risk profile report (category D) records the client's knowledge and understanding: no understanding of stocks and shares, no understanding of EIS/VCTs, no understanding of pension planning and income in retirement, and no investment experience in these areas beyond cash savings in the last 5 years. The questionnaire produced ATR 4 (Cautious Balanced); the agreed level was overridden to 5 (Balanced). No vulnerability assessment section or outcome appears anywhere in the risk profile document. The fact find (category B) records a preferred delivery method of 'No Preference' and shows a declaration signed on 19/09/2025 but contains no vulnerability screening section, no record of any support needs, no communication preferences beyond 'No preference', and no third-party authority arrangements beyond the suitability report's own mention of Power of Attorney as a prudent future step. No Power of Attorney or trusted contact is on record. File notes (category C) contain research notes on fund risk ratings and alternative options but no vulnerability observations. The cashflow and planning documents (category F) contain no vulnerability-related content. Across all 62 retrieved passages, there is no vulnerability assessment, no screening outcome, and no record that FG21/1 drivers were considered for either client.

    Discrepancies:

      - The report asserts foreseeable harms as mitigated but no formal vulnerability assessment outcome exists anywhere in the file for either client (C1 or C2), meaning the absence of vulnerability is asserted rather than evidenced.

      - The client's knowledge questionnaire responses record no understanding of pension planning, stocks and shares, or investment markets, and no investment experience in these products — these are low-capability indicators under FG21/1 that are not reflected as vulnerability drivers in the report's reasoning.

      - The ATR override from 4 to 5 combined with stated low financial knowledge and no investment experience represents a compound capability/risk indicator that the report does not frame within the FG21/1 vulnerability framework.

      - The report mentions Power of Attorney as prudent but records no existing PoA, no trusted contact, and no third-party authority arrangement — the file contains nothing to support or contradict this, leaving the client's position on PoA unrecorded as a vulnerability safeguard.

      - No communication preferences, accessible format requirements, or adjustments to the advice process are recorded anywhere in the file despite 'No Preference' being the only notation in the fact find — it is unclear whether preferences were actively explored or simply defaulted.

      - The extraction confirms /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome are NotPresentInDocument, meaning the report is silent on vulnerability screening, not merely that extraction failed.

      - The suitability report's foreseeable harms are inferred constructs (one explicitly flagged as 'Inferred' by the extraction) rather than the output of a documented vulnerability and foreseeable harm assessment process.

    The check requires that where vulnerability was assessed and recorded, the report's description of support needs, communication and adjustments reflects it, and the recommendation accounts for identified characteristics. Here, the threshold question is whether vulnerability was assessed at all. The extraction confirms that /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome are NotPresentInDocument — this is report silence, not extraction failure. No vulnerability screening section appears in the risk profile report, the fact find, the file notes, or any other document in the pack. The file carries several FG21/1 low-capability drivers: the client has no self-reported understanding of pension planning, stocks and shares, or investment markets; no investment experience outside cash savings; and the ATR questionnaire was overridden upward despite this low capability profile. These are not age indicators alone (the age guard does not apply here) — they are direct capability and comprehension indicators that FG21/1 identifies as potential vulnerability drivers. The report does not acknowledge them as such and does not record any process by which they were screened and found not to constitute vulnerability. The two foreseeable harms recorded in the canonical model are cost and investment risk harms — legitimate disclosures — but they are not a substitute for a vulnerability overlay. The Power of Attorney mention is forward-looking advice, not a record that the client's current capacity or support needs were assessed. The 'No Preference' communication field in the fact find is insufficient to evidence active exploration of communication needs. Because no vulnerability assessment exists anywhere in the case despite clear low-capability FG21/1 drivers (low financial knowledge, no investment experience, ATR override upward), the overlay was never applied. This meets the High severity threshold: no vulnerability assessment in a case with clear FG21/1 drivers.

  [G10.5] Vulnerabilities recorded in the file and in the report

  Potential Concern — severity High

    Report:   The canonical model records /vulnerability/perClient[]/hasVulnerability as 'NotAssessed' for both clients. The sub-paths for categories, characteristics and assessedOn are absent from the extracted model, and the extraction metadata confirms these paths were NotPresentInDocument — meaning the report is silent on vulnerability assessment for both clients.

    File:     The fact find (P10) records 'Accessible format requirement: No Requirement' and preferred delivery method as 'No Preference' (P7). The fact find (P4) records the meeting as a 'Video Call - Recorded' with nobody else present. The risk profile report (P13) notes no additional notes were recorded for investor experience or capacity for loss questionnaire discussions. The file notes (P1) address ATR and capacity for loss in substantive terms but contain no explicit vulnerability assessment, no recording of FG21/1 driver characteristics, and no mention of support needs, communication adjustments, third-party involvement, Power of Attorney or trusted contacts. The cashflow modelling document (P19) records client ages (69 and 65) and life events but no vulnerability indicators. No passage in the pack contains a completed vulnerability assessment, a vulnerability screening question, or any record that vulnerability was considered and found absent.

    Discrepancies:

      - The report records hasVulnerability as 'NotAssessed' for both clients — this is not a finding of 'no vulnerability' but a statement that no assessment was performed. No document in the file evidences that a vulnerability assessment was conducted, nor that the adviser considered and documented the outcome of a FG21/1 screening exercise.

      - Client 1 (C1) is recorded as aged 70 (DOB 07/06/1956) in the fact find (P3). Age 70 is not itself a vulnerability characteristic, but it is a recognised FG21/1 driver (life stage) that should prompt a documented consideration of vulnerability; no such consideration appears anywhere in the file.

      - The monthly cash flow shows a deficit of £-288.00 (P9), which is a potential low financial resilience indicator under FG21/1. No vulnerability or capacity-related note addresses this in the context of FG21/1 screening.

      - The risk profile report explicitly records 'No additional notes were recorded' for both investor experience and capacity for loss discussions (P13), indicating no vulnerability-related observations were captured at the risk profiling stage.

      - No third-party authority, Power of Attorney, trusted contact or representative is referenced in either the report or the file — absence is consistent across both sides but no positive confirmation that this was considered and confirmed as not applicable is recorded.

    The extraction metadata confirms that /vulnerability/C1/assessmentOutcome and /vulnerability/C2/assessmentOutcome were NotPresentInDocument — this is report silence, not extraction failure. The report therefore makes no positive assertion about vulnerability; it simply records 'NotAssessed'. The file equally contains no vulnerability screening record, no completed assessment, and no documented conclusion that neither client has vulnerability characteristics. Under FG21/1 and PRIN 2A, firms must consider vulnerability for all retail clients, not only those who present obvious indicators. The absence of any assessment record — in either the report or the supporting file — is itself the concern. The client is aged 70 (a life-stage driver), has a monthly cash flow deficit (a financial resilience driver), and the advice involves pension consolidation affecting retirement income. These are clear FG21/1 prompts. The guard that 'age alone is not vulnerability' applies: the finding is not that age 70 equals vulnerability, but that no documented consideration of any FG21/1 driver appears anywhere. The guard about a resolved or historic vulnerability does not apply as no vulnerability of any kind is recorded. The 'both absent' decision rule in the guard set states 'No issue on this requirement; no vulnerability is recorded anywhere' — however, that rule applies where there are genuinely no drivers present. Here, objective FG21/1 drivers (age, cash flow deficit, life event of approaching/at retirement) exist in the file and no screening was conducted or documented. The correct outcome is therefore Potential Concern rather than No Issue, because the file carries clear FG21/1 drivers that should have prompted a documented vulnerability consideration, and neither the report nor any supporting document shows that this occurred.

FIGURES DESCRIBED DIFFERENTLY IN DIFFERENT CHECKS
